Motivation: To understand how thermal properties of quantum many-body states vary with the choice of observables and operations, and to use these insights for efficient work extraction.

Method: The study investigates the thermodynamic hierarchy by examining how different observables and allowed operations influence the thermal status of isolated quantum many-body states.

Result: The research establishes a connection between the increased operational access and the mutual-information density, leading to a quantifiable work gain that is limited by the entropy difference at various levels of the thermodynamic hierarchy.

Conclusion: The potential for enhanced work extraction in quantum many-body systems is contingent upon understanding and utilizing the hierarchical structure of thermodynamic observables and operations.

Abstract: Thermodynamics is operational in the sense that the very concept of thermal equilibrium depends crucially on the choice of observables, while the amount of extractable work is defined relative to the allowed operations. Here we show that isolated quantum many-body states admit a thermodynamic hierarchical structure of observables and allowed operations, where the same state can be thermal at one level of the hierarchy and athermal at another. Enlarging the set of allowed operations therefore renders such hidden athermality a potential resource for work extraction; the resulting work gain is bounded in terms of the difference between the entropy densities of the two levels. This entropy difference takes the form of the mutual-information density. We establish the bounds for three extensions of operational access: increased spatial resolution, increased duration of control, and nonlocal connectivity, which provide access to position-state Holevo information, correlations between neighboring regions and spatially nonlocal correlations, respectively. Thus the difference between entropies at different levels of the thermodynamic hierarchy governs the bound on the work gain associated with moving to a less restricted level.

</details>


### [128] [Protected Logical Qudits in Kitaev Quantum Double Models via Stable Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31011)
*Naihong Hu,Futao Wang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study presents a framework for constructing logical qudits in fault-tolerant quantum computation using representation theory, focusing on finite groups and achieving specific logical states for certain groups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for fault-tolerance in quantum computing and robust protection of encoded information.

Method: A representation-theoretic framework is developed, employing $	heta$-stable irreducible representations to establish criteria and derive commutation relations for logical qudits in group-based models.

Result: Logical qubits are obtained for symmetric groups and a logical qutrit for the alternating group, along with a method to realize qudits of arbitrary dimension via a specific group construction.

Conclusion: The findings enhance the toolkit for fault-tolerant quantum computing, enabling logical operations and computations for higher-dimensional qudits.

Abstract: Fault-tolerant quantum computation requires robust protection of encoded quantum information. In this work, we develop a representation-theoretic framework for constructing protected logical qudits in finite-group Kitaev quantum double models. By introducing $\varepsilon$-stable irreducible representations, we establish a necessary and sufficient existence criterion and derive ribbon--projector commutation relations that yield a $d$-dimensional protected logical subspace. We apply this construction to symmetric and alternating groups, obtaining logical qubits for $S_n$ ($n\ge 3$) and a logical qutrit for $A_4$. Moreover, the family $(\mathbb Z_2)^d\rtimes\mathbb Z_d$ realizes protected logical qudits of arbitrary dimension $d\ge 2$. Finally, for $D(A_4)$, we describe a scheme for universal logical qutrit computation using ribbon-based logical operations.

</details>


### [129] [Entanglement and magic transitions in an all-to-all non-Hermitian spin model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31064)
*Sasanka Dowarah,Michael Kolodrubetz*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the dominant eigenvalue in non-Hermitian many-body systems, revealing transitions in eigenstates influenced by disorder types.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the long-time states in non-Hermitian many-body systems, particularly how disorder affects dominant eigenvalues and state properties.

Method: Construction of a non-Hermitian spin ensemble with $k$-local all-to-all interactions, analyzing the effect of different types of disorder on the dominant eigenvalue and eigenstates.

Result: Identified transitions in eigenstates from outlier to bulk states under various disorder conditions, with distinct behaviors for anti-Hermitian and Hermitian disorders, establishing mathematical relationships for transition thresholds.

Conclusion: The findings illustrate significant connections between spectral properties of non-Hermitian systems and measurement-induced transitions in quantum mechanics.

Abstract: The long-time state of a non-Hermitian system is determined by the eigenvalue with the largest imaginary part. In interacting many-body systems this eigenvalue usually cannot be tracked analytically, and the character of the state it selects is unknown. We construct a non-Hermitian spin ensemble of $L$ spins with exactly $k$-local all-to-all interactions, in which this dominant eigenvalue can be tracked analytically from the clean limit into the disordered regime. Disorder produces a competition between an isolated spectral outlier and the edge of a many-body spectral bulk. We study three cases: purely anti-Hermitian disorder, purely Hermitian disorder, and mixed disorder of equal strength. For purely anti-Hermitian and mixed disorder, we show that when the bulk overtakes the outlier in imaginary part, the dominant eigenstate switches from an outlier state with low entanglement and magic (nonstabilizerness) to a bulk state with substantially larger entanglement and magic, with both changing at the same threshold. For $k \gg \sqrt{L}$ the bulk has a sharp spectral edge and the transition thresholds follow in closed form, while for $k \ll \sqrt{L}$ spectral tails broaden the transition into a crossover. Purely Hermitian disorder provides a contrasting case with no outlier-to-bulk switching. Finally, we map the non-Hermitian evolution exactly onto postselected trajectories of a monitored quantum system, connecting this spectral mechanism to measurement-induced transitions.

</details>


### [130] [Deterministic Minimum-Output-Entropy Nonadditivity via Haagerup's Inequality and Near-Free Permutation Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31081)
*Guocheng Zhen,Chengkai Zhu,Ranyiliu Chen,Xin Wang*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper provides a deterministic algorithm producing specific permutations on points related to minimum-output-entropy nonadditivity, demonstrating a significant entropy gap between channels and establishing conditions for self-tensor superadditivity in quantum information.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the minimum-output-entropy nonadditivity phenomenon and improve understanding of quantum channels in relation to mixed-unitary proofs.

Method: A deterministic polynomial-time algorithm outputs K permutations on nearly N points, using spectral techniques and relating quantum channel properties to entropy measures.

Result: Shows a positive entropy gap under certain conditions, revealing the intricate geometry of output channels and their correlations with quantum information measures.

Conclusion: Demonstrates critical links between deterministic entropy gaps, self-tensor superadditivity, and the geometry of quantum output bodies, suggesting areas for further research.

Abstract: We give a deterministic realization of the finite-dimensional quadratic certificate underlying Collins's mixed-unitary proof of minimum-output-entropy nonadditivity. For every fixed integer $K\ge 2$ and rational $η>0$ satisfying $\log K>2(3+η)^2$, a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm, for every sufficiently large target size $N$, outputs $K$ permutations on $N'=N+o_{K,η}(N)$ points. Restricting their permutation matrices to the nontrivial standard representation yields real orthogonal Stinespring blocks and a channel $Φ_{N'}:M_{N'-1}(\mathbb{C})\to M_K(\mathbb{C})$ such that \[ 2H_{\min}(Φ_{N'}) -H_{\min}(Φ_{N'}^{\otimes 2}) \ge \frac{\log K}{K} -2\log\left(1+\frac{(3+η)^2}{K}\right) >0. \] The construction combines Haagerup's length-two inequality with the simultaneous deterministic spectral approximation of O'Donnell and Wu. We further show that the constant $3$ is asymptotically sharp on the relevant Hermitian zero-diagonal coefficient class and that the finite spectral transfer is nearly saturated, thereby isolating the finer geometry of the full output body as the natural next level of refinement beyond the scalar-radius method. Finally, a standard covariant extension converts the same deterministic entropy gap exactly into self-tensor superadditivity of the one-shot Holevo quantity.

</details>


### [131] [Phase-noise induced many-body interference suppression in Gaussian Boson Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31089)
*Dario Cilluffo,Matthias Kost,Martin B. Plenio*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a tensor-network approach to Gaussian Boson Sampling (GBS), connecting output probabilities to experimental data while analyzing the effects of phase diffusion on quantum interference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a clearer understanding of GBS output statistics and their relationship to experimentally measurable quantities and phase fluctuations.

Method: A Heisenberg-picture tensor-network formulation is introduced, leading to a Fock-space expression for output probabilities that differentiates between phase-insensitive contributions and interference sectors from matchings.

Result: The framework recovers the Hafnian structure, elucidating how phase diffusion affects many-body interference and shifts the output statistics towards more classical behaviors.

Conclusion: This work offers a systematic way to link phase fluctuations in experiments with the degradation of quantum interference, enhancing the understanding of photonic quantum sampling.

Abstract: We develop a Heisenberg-picture tensor-network formulation of collision-free Gaussian Boson Sampling, providing a direct Fock-space expression for output probabilities in terms of experimentally accessible quantities. The resulting representation naturally recovers the Hafnian structure while revealing the decomposition of GBS probability into a phase-insensitive contribution and a hierarchy of interference sectors associated with pairs of perfect matchings. As an application, we investigate phase diffusion and show how it progressively suppresses many-body interference, driving the output statistics toward a classical dimer-model regime. Our results establish a transparent framework for connecting experimentally characterized phase fluctuations with the loss of quantum interference in photonic quantum sampling experiments.

</details>


### [132] [Tunable Exceptional Points for Quantum Sensing in a Spin--Orbit-Angular-Momentum Coupled BEC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31095)
*Zicheng Zhang,Xi-Wang Luo*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores a tunable sensing platform using exceptional points in a Bose-Einstein condensate to enhance quantum sensing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve quantum-limited sensing by utilizing exceptional points while addressing noise issues in non-Hermitian systems.

Method: The authors analyze a Bogoliubov dynamical matrix derived from a Hermitian Hamiltonian, identifying multiple exceptional points through tunable parameters and mapping their stability characteristics.

Result: Significant enhancement of quantum Fisher information is observed when approaching exceptional points from a stable regime, with a method for comparing sensing performance based on gap-opening rates.

Conclusion: Tuning second-order exceptional points can yield linear enhancements in quantum Fisher information and provide a viable pathway for experimental applications in quantum sensing.

Abstract: Exceptional points (EPs) can induce strongly amplified responses to weak perturbations, but enhanced spectral sensitivity in conventional non-Hermitian systems does not necessarily translate into improved quantum-limited sensing because of the associated gain and loss noise. Here, we investigate a tunable EP sensing platform based on the intrinsic non-Hermitian Bogoliubov dynamics of a spin-orbit-angular-momentum-coupled Bose-Einstein condensate. Starting from a fully Hermitian microscopic Hamiltonian, we derive a Bogoliubov dynamical matrix that exhibits parity-time (PT) symmetry, with EPs tunable through the Raman coupling and interaction parameters. We identify multiple EPs and map their trajectories and associated stability landscapes, revealing strong quantum Fisher information enhancement when the EPs are approached from the PT-symmetric stable regime. We further find that the gap-opening rate around a second-order EP provides a useful relative indicator for comparing the sensing performance of EPs, while higher-order EPs are not necessarily accessible from the stable regime. Moreover, two second-order EPs can be tuned to overlap, allowing their sensing contributions to add and yielding a linear enhancement of the total quantum Fisher information. Finally, we show that a spin-density measurement can approach the quantum Fisher information limit, providing an experimentally accessible route to tunable EP-enhanced quantum sensing in Bose-Einstein condensates.

</details>


### [133] [Unconditional Certified Randomness without Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31112)
*Andrea Coladangelo,Dakshita Khurana,Saachi Mutreja,Bhaskar Roberts,Joseph Slote,Avishay Tal*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a certified randomness protocol in the quantum random oracle model that is non-interactive, publicly verifiable, and secure against adaptive quantum queries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a robust method for certified randomness that does not depend on conjectures and is secure against strong adversaries.

Method: Develop a non-interactive protocol based on Yamakawa and Zhandry's proof, ensuring it is verifiable by a classical verifier.

Result: Demonstrated unconditional security against subexponentially-many adaptive quantum queries to the random oracle, improving upon prior work.

Conclusion: The proposed protocol overcomes limitations of previous approaches by ensuring security without reliance on conjectures or specific adversarial constraints.

Abstract: We obtain a certified randomness protocol in the quantum random oracle model. The protocol is non-interactive and publicly verifiable with a classical verifier, and is based on Yamakawa and Zhandry's proof of quantumness [JACM'24]. We prove unconditional security of this protocol against adversaries making subexponentially-many adaptive quantum queries to the random oracle.
  Prior work on certified randomness relative to a random oracle additionally assumed the Aaronson--Ambainis conjecture or proved security only against low query-depth adversaries.

</details>


### [134] ["Train classical, deploy quantum" requires rethinking generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31117)
*Snehal Raj,Natansh Mathur,Alejandro Perdomo-Ortiz*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: Quantum generative models trained classically with moment-matching loss show poorer generalization than likelihood-trained models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the effectiveness of training quantum generative models and assess their generalization capabilities using moment-matching loss versus likelihood-based methods.

Method: The study benchmarks various quantum and classical generative models, employing direct sampling to evaluate their performance on generalization using two distinct datasets.

Result: Models trained with moment-matching loss demonstrated worse generalization performance compared to those trained using likelihood-based objectives.

Conclusion: Moment-matching loss is not a reliable measure of generalization, necessitating new strategies for managing train-classical, deploy-quantum workflows focused on enhancing generalization.

Abstract: Generative models have become central across science and industry, from image and text synthesis to the design of molecules and materials. Quantum generative models are considered one of the most promising applications for quantum computers, since a quantum circuit naturally produces samples from the distribution it encodes, and for suitable circuits that distribution is believed to be hard for any classical computer to reproduce. A leading strategy trains these models on a classical computer and reserves the quantum device for generating samples at deployment. This is possible when the training loss can be evaluated on a classical computer. A prime example is the maximum mean discrepancy (MMD$^2$), a moment-matching loss that compares the model and the data through their Pauli-$Z$ correlations. Research so far has asked whether such models can be trained and whether their sampling is hard; whether minimizing such an objective yields a model that generalizes, rather than one that merely reproduces the training statistics, remains poorly understood. We benchmark a broad set of quantum and classical generative models by direct sampling and show that models trained with a moment-matching loss generally show worse generalization than the likelihood-trained models. We show this on two application-inspired datasets: first a cardinality-constrained dataset at up to $30$ qubits and second a dataset of genomic single-nucleotide variants, whose valid set is the observed data. These results indicate that a converged moment-matching loss is not a reliable measure of generalization, and that train-classical, deploy-quantum workflows will need approaches that target generalization directly, leaving open whether better training objectives suffice or whether the model architectures themselves must change.

</details>


### [135] [Engineering multi-photon dissipation with a dc-voltage-biased Josephson junction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31154)
*Marco Paradina,Ambroise Peugeot,Roberto Negrin,Oscar Novat,Tristan Villain,Anil Murani,Jean-Loup Ville,Sébastien Jezouin,Raphaël Lescanne,Audrey Bienfait,Benjamin Huard*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a method to achieve multi-photon dissipation using a dc-voltage-biased SQUID, which eliminates spurious nonlinearities, allowing for the conversion of high-Q mode photons into a lossy mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve multi-photon dissipation for bosonic qubits without the detrimental effects of spurious nonlinear terms commonly introduced by traditional methods.

Method: Engineering dissipation through a dc-voltage-biased SQUID, enabling conversion of multiple photons from a high-Q mode to a single photon in a lossy mode, characterized via Wigner tomography.

Result: Successful activation of dissipation for one, two, or four photons, demonstrated through Wigner tomography, highlighting the effectiveness of the dc-biased junctions.

Conclusion: Dc-voltage-biased junctions are established as valuable for reservoir engineering and effective in stabilizing cat-qubits.

Abstract: Multi-photon dissipation -- a key resource for bosonic qubits -- is usually realized by parametrically pumping a Josephson coupler at the cost of spurious nonlinear terms. Here we instead engineer it using a dc-voltage-biased SQUID, such that these parasitic terms average out. We activate the conversion of one, two, or four photons of a high-Q mode into a single photon of a lossy mode. We characterize the two-photon dissipation by Wigner tomography, establishing dc-biased junctions as a resource for reservoir engineering and a viable route to cat-qubit stabilization.

</details>


### [136] [Bosonic codes from compact phase spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31156)
*David Roberts,Aaron Slipper,Alireza Parhizkar,Victor V. Albert,Mohammad Hafezi*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores bosonic quantum error-correcting codes on genus-two Riemann surfaces, highlighting a no-go theorem that limits the existence of normalizable quantum states under certain conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the algebraic structures of bosonic quantum error-correcting codes and their implications in quantum information theory.

Method: The authors construct code words as automorphic forms and generate code spaces analytically across various weights, while proving the no-go theorem regarding non-amenability of the stabilizer group.

Result: The study confirms that for genus greater than one, the stabilizer group is non-amenable, resulting in a positive spectral gap in the stabilizer Hamiltonian, thereby preventing normalizable quantum states from satisfying all stabilizer conditions.

Conclusion: This work reveals significant differences between bosonic codes on higher genus surfaces and traditional GKP codes, underscoring restrictions in achieving stable quantum states due to the properties of stabilizer groups.

Abstract: We present the algebraic structure of bosonic quantum error-correcting codes on genus-two Riemann surfaces. We explicitly construct the code words as automorphic forms and analytically generate the full tower of code spaces at all weights. We prove a fundamental no-go theorem: for any genus greater than one, the stabilizer group is non-amenable, forcing a strictly positive spectral gap in the stabilizer Hamiltonian. Consequently, no normalizable quantum state can satisfy all stabilizer conditions. This sharply contrasts with standard Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) codes, where the amenability of the stabilizer group $\mathbb{Z}^2$ permits approximate code words with arbitrary precision.

</details>


### [137] [Efficient search for excitable zero-modes in constrained systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31165)
*Jean-Yves Desaules*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This work presents a method for efficiently finding analytical expressions for excitations in kinetically constrained systems, particularly focusing on Rydberg atom arrays, leading to insights into zero-modes and many-body scars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kinetically constrained systems exhibit unique properties like quantum many-body scars and large zero-mode spaces, warranting exploration of their eigenstates.

Method: An efficient protocol is developed for identifying translation-invariant excitable zero-modes through eigendecomposition of the local unconstrained Hamiltonian.

Result: The method generates a continuous matrix-product-state manifold entirely within the zero-energy eigenspace, which expands exponentially with the system size and fully occupies the zero-momentum eigenspace for odd sizes. It also yields analytical expressions for zero-modes at non-zero momentum and specific energy scars.

Conclusion: The findings enhance the understanding of many-body states in constrained systems, demonstrating fruitful connections between the local eigenbasis and the physical properties of the system.

Abstract: Kinetically constrained systems, such as those representing Rydberg atom arrays in the blockade regime, have gathered considerable attention due to the presence of atypical eigenstates in their spectrum. The latter manifests itself through the presence of quantum many-body scars as well as unusually large zero-mode (ZM) subspaces which contain analytically tractable eigenstates with various entanglement scalings. In particular, some of the latter are excitable zero-modes (EZMs), meaning that they can be promoted to a non-zero energy for open boundary conditions. In this work, I present an efficient protocol for finding analytical expressions for translation-invariant EZMs in constrained systems, based on the eigendecomposition of the local unconstrained Hamiltonian. I demonstrate the power of this method on a decorated Rydberg chain. In that model, my protocol directly produces a continuous matrix-product-state manifold located entirely in the zero-energy eigenspace for periodic boundary conditions. The span of that manifold grows exponentially with the system, and for odd system sizes it covers the entire zero-momentum eigenspace with zero energy. I then show how the physical structure of the manifold, which is tied to the local eigenbasis, also allows one to derive analytical expressions for ZMs at non-zero momentum and for a polynomial number of exact scars at $E=\pm\sqrt{3}$.

</details>

### [138] [A Pythia8 Tune for Open Charm and Beauty Production in Fixed-Target Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29076)
*Matei Climescu,Didar Dobur,Kirill Skovpen*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: FTFT is a set of optimized Pythia8 parameters for charm and beauty hadron production in fixed-target collisions, crucial for predicting neutrino fluxes in experiments like SHiP.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate heavy-flavour production is essential to predict neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields in beam-dump experiments.

Method: The FTFT tune is derived by fitting differential distributions of $D$ mesons to data and scaling with $K$-factors from inclusive charm cross-section measurements.

Result: The fitted parameters for charm fragmentation and multiparton interactions differ from standard LHC-tuned defaults, aligning with previous fixed-target studies.

Conclusion: The new FTFT parameters improve the simulation of heavy-flavour production in fixed-target experiments, enhancing the reliability of predictions for neutrino-related research.

Abstract: We present FTFT (Fixed-Target Fragmentation Tune), a set of Pythia8 parameters optimised for the production of charm and beauty hadrons in fixed-target collisions at centre-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s} \simeq 20-42$ GeV. Accurate heavy-flavour production in this regime is needed to predict neutrino fluxes and hidden-particle yields at beam-dump experiments such as SHiP. The tune is derived in two stages. First, differential distributions in Feynman-$x$ ($x_F$), transverse momentum squared ($p_T^2$), and charge or leading-particle production asymmetry of $D$ mesons are fitted to data from pion- and proton-beam fixed-target experiments. Second, $K$-factors for each beam are extracted from inclusive charm cross-section measurements to scale the simulated cross section to the measured data. The fitted charm-fragmentation and multiparton-interaction parameters depart from standard LHC-tuned defaults, in line with earlier fixed-target studies.

</details>


### [139] [Sensitivity of a Missing-Mass Search for a Light Dark Photon with a Positron Beam on a Hydrogen Target](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29159)
*Weizhi Xiong,Ashot Gasparian,Bogdan Wojtsekhowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This study evaluates the sensitivity of detecting light dark photons using a positron beam and liquid hydrogen, highlighting the use of missing-mass techniques and projected sensitivity outcomes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to contribute to research and development for a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab, focusing on the detection of light dark photons.

Method: It employs a Geant4-based simulation, utilizing a positron beam on a liquid-hydrogen target and high-resolution calorimeter for photons, applying the missing-mass technique in the process $e^+e^- \to γA'$.

Result: The expected backgrounds from standard QED processes are assessed, and the projected sensitivity to the kinetic-mixing parameter $ε^2$ is analyzed as a function of the dark photon mass, specifically showing sensitivity at $4×10^{-8}$ for $m_{A'}=19$ MeV.

Conclusion: The experiment with a 500 MeV beam effectively targets the $A'$ mass range of 7-19 MeV, indicating a robust framework for potential discovery or constraints on light dark photon interactions.

Abstract: We present a Geant4-based simulation study of the sensitivity to a light dark photon, $A'$, using a positron beam incident on a thin liquid-hydrogen target and a high-resolution forward electromagnetic calorimeter for final-state photon reconstruction. This study is motivated by ongoing research and development toward a low-energy positron facility at Jefferson Lab. The proposed search employs the missing-mass technique in the annihilation-in-flight process $e^+e^- \to γA'$, providing sensitivity to the $A'$ independently of its decay mode. We evaluate the expected backgrounds from standard QED annihilation and bremsstrahlung processes and present the projected sensitivity to the kinetic-mixing parameter $ε^2$ as a function of the $A'$ mass. The experiment with 500~MeV beam will provide the data for $A'$ mass range 7-19 MeV. The projected sensitivity on $ε^2$ is $4\times10^{-8}$ at $m_{A'}=19$~MeV.

</details>


### [140] [Evidence for the dead-cone effect in bottom quark initiated jets produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29412)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The study analyzes bottom quark jets in proton-proton collisions to investigate the dead-cone effect, finding strong evidence that models incorporating this effect better match experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the impact of quark mass effects, specifically the dead-cone effect, on parton showers in high-energy collisions.

Method: Analyzed a CMS proton-proton collision data set at $[0m[0m\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV, focusing on the emission density of bottom quark jets as a function of angular separation between subjets, specifically in the context of top quark pair production.

Result: Data strongly favors a Monte Carlo model that accounts for the dead-cone effect, showing a significance of 4.3 standard deviations compared to a model that does not include this effect.

Conclusion: The dead-cone effect is a significant factor in understanding the jet structure of bottom quark jets, highlighting the importance of quark mass in parton shower dynamics.

Abstract: The suppression of collinear gluon emissions from a massive quark, or the ``dead-cone effect,'' is the primary manifestation of quark mass effects in parton showers. A proton-proton collision data set recorded by the CMS experiment at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV with an integrated luminosity of 59.8 fb$^{-1}$ is analyzed. A differential measurement is presented of the emission density of bottom quark jets, studied as a function of the angular separation between iteratively declustered subjets. The measurement is performed in bottom quark initiated jets with transverse momenta between 40 and 200 GeV originating from top quark pair production. Compared to data, a parton shower Monte Carlo model that includes the dead-cone effect is favored, while a model without the dead-cone effect is disfavored with a significance of 4.3 standard deviations.

</details>

Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The paper presents the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos using elastic neutrino-electron scattering, establishing a new low-energy threshold and demonstrating the capabilities of liquid xenon detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To measure low-energy solar neutrinos and establish a lower detection threshold, enhancing the understanding of neutrino properties and interactions.

Method: Utilized data from the XENONnT experiment, analyzing elastic scattering events in the energy range of 1 keV to 140 keV, with a total exposure of 2.46 t·y.

Result: Measured a solar $pp$ neutrino flux of $(10.2 \\pm 2.0) 	imes 10^{10}$ cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$ with a significance of $5.0σ$, rejecting the background-only hypothesis.

Conclusion: The results indicate a significant detection of solar neutrinos and highlight the potential of liquid xenon detectors for advancing neutrino physics at keV energies.

Abstract: We report on the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos through elastic neutrino-electron scattering in a dark matter experiment, establishing the lowest energy threshold for any neutrino detection to date. The measurement utilizes data from the first two science runs of XENONnT, corresponding to an exposure of 2.46 t $\cdot$ y, and covers electron recoil energies between 1 keV and 140 keV, providing sensitivity to solar neutrinos with energies down to 17 keV. We reject the background-only hypothesis with a statistical significance of $5.0σ$ and measure a solar $pp$ neutrino flux of $(10.2 \pm 2.0) \times 10^{10}$ cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$. The result is larger, but statistically consistent with the previous measurement by Borexino at $1.9σ$. Together with recent observations of coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering of $^8$B solar neutrinos in XENONnT and other liquid-xenon time projection chambers, these results demonstrate the growing potential of liquid xenon detectors for neutrino physics down to the keV-scale and represent an important milestone towards a next-generation multipurpose observatory.

</details>


### [142] [From a Long-standing Prediction to a New Family of Hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29502)
*Xining Wang,Kai Yi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The article reviews the evolution of fully charmed tetraquarks, highlighting key contributions from NNU and THU teams at the CMS experiment, and explores their implications for hadron spectroscopy and Quantum Chromodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document the progress and breakthroughs in the study of fully charmed tetraquarks and their significance in understanding hadron spectroscopy.

Method: Review of theoretical and experimental developments, with a focus on contributions from NNU and THU at the CMS experiment.

Result: Discovery of the fully charmed tetraquark family, establishment of resonance interference, and determination of their quantum numbers.

Conclusion: These advances have shifted focus from seeking individual exotic resonances to studying a new family of hadrons, enhancing the understanding of nonperturbative Quantum Chromodynamics.

Abstract: Fully charmed tetraquarks have evolved from a long-standing theoretical prediction into one of the most active frontiers in hadron spectroscopy. This article reviews their development from early theoretical studies and experimental searches to the recent breakthroughs at the Large Hadron Collider, with particular emphasis on the contributions of the Nanjing Normal University (NNU) and Tsinghua University (THU) teams at the CMS experiment. We summarize the discovery of the fully charmed tetraquark family, the establishment of interference among resonances, and the first determination of their quantum numbers. These advances have transformed the search for a single exotic resonance into the study of a new family of hadrons and opened a promising avenue for exploring the nonperturbative dynamics of Quantum Chromodynamics.

</details>


### [143] [Low-energy Muon-Nucleon scattering experiment: LUNE (White Paper)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29509)
*Chenlei An,Dong Bai,Ziyu Bai,Kai Chen,Liangwen Chen,Xiang Chen,Jianqiao Deng,Yanxin Dou,Yicheng Feng,Zekai Feng,Lu Gao,Chang Gong,Aiqiang Guo,Liang Han,Qundong Han,Defu Hou,Ruiwen Hou,Huigang Hu,Chen Ji,Xiangdong Ji,Vijay Kumar,Dikai Li,Jiuzhao Li,Liang Li,Qite Li,Xin-Qiang Li,Yuan Li,Qiming Liang,Yutie Liang,Dong Liu,Duanqing Liu,Langtian Liu,Weijie Liu,Zejia Lu,Maowu Nie,Ziwen Pan,Hua Pei,Jinkang Peng,Shusu Shi,Qintao Song,Xiaocheng Song,Xiangming Sun,Yichen Sun,Zhiyu Sun,Enke Wang,Fei Wang,Hulin Wang,Jike Wang,Xiang-Peng Wang,Xiao Wang,Xin-Nian Wang,Yangxi Wang,Yaping Wang,Zeren Simon Wang,Yanbing Wei,Yuehong Xie,Weizhi Xiong,Nu Xu,Yu Xu,Siqi Yang,Yadong Yang,Hang Yin,Xing-Bo Yuan,Dongliang Zhang,Ranyu Zhang,Ruitian Zhang,Xueheng Zhang,Yu Zhang,Yuxiang Zhao,Zihan Zhao,Shuai Zhou,XiaoKang Zhou,Xiaoyu Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The HIAF will produce high-quality muon beams for precision scattering measurements, addressing key questions in nuclear and particle physics through the LUNE Collaboration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To tackle fundamental issues in nuclear and particle physics, such as the proton charge radius puzzle and enhance our understanding of electromagnetic structures and beyond-Standard-Model phenomena.

Method: Utilize high-quality muon beams from HIAF for precision measurements in elastic scattering and studies of quantum electrodynamics and hadronic interactions, progressing from basic measurements to more complex ones in two phases.

Result: Achieve approximately 1.0% precision in determining the proton charge radius and perform systematic studies of proton electromagnetic form factors, with applications in understanding two-photon exchange and other fundamental interactions.

Conclusion: The LUNE program will provide significant insights into both nucleon and nuclear structures while exploring beyond-Standard-Model physics, presented in a detailed white paper outlining its scientific rationale and methodologies.

Abstract: The HIAF will provide high-intensity, high-quality muon beams with momenta from 0.5 to 7.5 GeV/c. This energy range is uniquely suited for precision muon scattering, bridging the gap between low-energy electron facilities and future high-energy lepton-ion colliders. In particular, HIAF will enable precision measurements with both positive and negative muon beams over a broad kinematic range, complementing existing electron-scattering facilities such as JLab, EicC and EIC.
  Based on HIAF muon source, the LUNE Collaboration has been established to address several fundamental questions in nuclear and particle physics, including the proton charge radius puzzle, nucleon electromagnetic structure, and the dynamics of quantum electrodynamics and hadronic interactions. The program proceeds in two phases, from elastic scattering to nucleon structure and beyond-Standard-Model searches.
  The experiment is expected to determine the proton charge radius with a precision of approximately 1.0\% using elastic muon-proton scattering. It will also perform systematic measurements of the proton electromagnetic form factors with both $μ^+$ and $μ^-$ beams, enabling precise studies of two-photon exchange effects and stringent tests of quantum electrodynamics. Beyond elastic scattering, LUNE will investigate TMD, gravitational form factors, and nuclear charge radii, providing new insights into the 3D structure of nucleons and nuclei. The experiment will further address important topics including Coulomb-distortion corrections, nuclear medium effects, and possible signatures of physics beyond the Standard Model.
  This white paper presents the scientific motivation, detector concept, expected performance, and long-term strategy of LUNE.

</details>

Yu,T. Yuan,S. Yun-C{á}rcamo,A. Zander Jurowitzki,A. Zegarelli,S. Zhang,Z. Zhang,P. Zhelnin,P. Zilberman,C. Zilleruelo Ca{ñ}as*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The study places constraints on low-scale gravity scenarios based on neutrino data from IceCube, highlighting specific limits for various models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To tackle the hierarchy problem in particle physics and its implications in the neutrino sector.

Method: Analysis of 10.7 years of upward-going muon neutrino data from IceCube, focusing on energy characteristics and spectral distortions.

Result: Constraints on compactification radius of extra dimensions ($R 	extless 0.17 \, 	ext{μm}$) and lower limits on the number of Standard Model copies ($N 	extgreater 	ext{O}(400)$).

Conclusion: These findings provide some of the strongest constraints in current literature and explore new parameter spaces regarding low-scale gravity scenarios.

Abstract: The hierarchy problem remains an open question in particle physics. A number of theories that address this problem lower the fundamental scale of gravity, resulting in observable consequences in the neutrino sector. In this work, we place constraints on low-scale gravity scenarios using high-energy neutrinos observed with the IceCube Neutrino Observatory. The analysis is based on 10.7 years of upward-going muon neutrino data in the energy range from 0.5 to 100 TeV. In this energy range, the theories predict characteristic spectral distortions arising from matter effects when neutrinos propagate through Earth. In the context of large extra dimension models, we constrain the compactification radius of the largest extra dimension to $R \lesssim 0.17\,μ\mathrm{m}$ at $90\%$ confidence level for both normal and inverted neutrino mass ordering. For scenarios with multiple Standard Model copies, we obtain lower limits of up to $N \gtrsim \mathcal{O}(400)$, depending on the value of the lightest neutrino mass. In parts of the parameter space, these results constitute the strongest constraints in the literature to our knowledge, while in other regions they probe previously unexplored parameter space.

</details>


### [145] [ConCA: Concentration-Aware Channel Attention for Fine-Grained Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30183)
*Yu-Sheng Liu,Yu-Chen Tung*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The paper introduces Concentration-Aware Channel Attention (ConCA) for fine-grained visual recognition, outperforming existing models by combining mean and entropy in channel descriptors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of existing channel attention mechanisms in fine-grained visual recognition, particularly their reliance on global average pooling, which fails to capture spatial details.

Method: ConCA combines activation mean with a negative-input entropy descriptor using softmax, processed through a lightweight depthwise 1-D convolutional MLP to generate per-channel attention weights.

Result: ConCA demonstrates improved performance over various baselines, including attention-free models and richer descriptor modules, across multiple fine-grained benchmarks and shows good generalization on iNat2021-mini.

Conclusion: The study highlights the significance of channel descriptors and their role in enhancing lightweight channel attention for fine-grained visual recognition.

Abstract: Lightweight channel attention mechanisms are widely used in image classification, yet their effectiveness in fine-grained visual recognition (FGVR) remains limited. Most modules summarize each channel by global average pooling (GAP), which captures activation magnitude but ignores spatial concentration, so channels with different spatial distributions but identical means receive the same descriptor. We propose Concentration-Aware Channel Attention (ConCA), which pairs the mean with a shift-invariant negative-input entropy (NegEnt), computed via a softmax over the negated activations, forming a dual descriptor that jointly encodes magnitude and concentration. A depthwise 1-D convolutional multi-layer perceptron (MLP), whose parameter count is linear in the number of channels, maps the pair to a per-channel weight. On six fine-grained benchmarks, ConCA improves over attention-free, SE-Net, and ECA-Net baselines as well as four richer descriptor-based modules under a controlled from-scratch protocol, and it generalizes across eight backbones on iNat2021-mini. These results indicate that the channel descriptor, together with the per-channel gating that maps it to attention weights, is an important but underexplored aspect of lightweight channel attention in FGVR.

</details>

Matsumoto,C. Ise,M. Ishitsuka,M. Sugo,M. Wako,K. Yamauchi,Y. Nakano,A. Yankelevich,F. Cormier,R. Gaur,M. Hartz,A. Konaka,X. Li,B. R. Smithers,S. Chen,Y. Wu,A. Q. Zhang,B. Zhang,H. Adhikary,M. Girgus,P. Govindaraj,M. Posiadala-Zezula,Y. S. Prabhu,S. B. Boyd,R. Edwards,D. Hadley,M. O'Flaherty,B. Richards,A. Ali,B. Jamieson,C. Bronner,D. Horiguchi,A. Minamino,Y. Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The study examined proton decay, finding no significant evidence and establishing stricter lifetime limits than past research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the phenomenon of proton decay, which is a key test of Grand Unified Theories in particle physics.

Method: The experiment utilized a substantial exposure of 401 kton·years during the pure water phase of Super-Kamiokande to search for the decay channels p→l+ + X.

Result: The research found no significant indications of proton decay, setting lower limits of 1.72×10^33 years for p→e+ + X and 0.61×10^33 years for p→μ+ + X at 90% confidence, improving limits by factors of 2 and 1.5, respectively.

Conclusion: The established limits contribute to the understanding of proton stability and constrain theories predicting proton decay.

Abstract: A search for proton decay via $p\rightarrow l^{+}+X$, where $l^{+}$ is a positively charged lepton and $X$ is an invisible, massless, neutral particle, was performed using a 401~kton$\cdot$years exposure representing the entire pure water phase of Super-Kamiokande. No significant indication of a proton decay was observed beyond the expected atmospheric neutrino background. Lower limits on the partial lifetime of the proton were set to at $1.72\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow e^{+}+X$ and $0.61\times10^{33}$ years for $p\rightarrow μ^{+}+X$ at the $90\%$ confidence level. These results improve on previous limits by factors of 2 and 1.5, respectively.

</details>

Zhang,J. Zhang,L. Zhang,Q. Z. Zhang,R. Zhang,S. Zhang,S. L. Zhang,Y. Zhang,Z. Zhang,J. Zhao,M. Zhao,Y. Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: The study measures the production cross-section ratio of $ψ(2S)$ to $J/ψ$ in proton-argon and proton-proton collisions, revealing nuclear effects in $p	ext{Ar}$ collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the production dynamics of charmonium states in different collision systems and establish a baseline for future experiments.

Method: The measurement is conducted using data from the LHCb experiment, comparing $p	ext{Ar}$ and $pp$ collisions, and evaluating the ratio as functions of transverse momentum and rapidity.

Result: The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ ratio in $p	ext{Ar}$ collisions is found to be $0.90 	ext{ ± } 0.06$ in a specific kinematic range, indicating nuclear effects.

Conclusion: This result provides critical insights for interpreting future measurements in larger collision systems by the LHCb experiment.

Abstract: A measurement of the $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is performed in proton-argon ($p\mathrm{Ar}$) and proton-proton ($pp$) collisions in fixed-target mode at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=113\,\mathrm{GeV}$. Data samples were collected by the LHCb experiment during argon and hydrogen gas injections in the SMOG2 storage cell, resulting in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ and $pp$ collisions, respectively. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ production cross-section ratio is measured as a function of the charmonium transverse momentum, $p_{\mathrm{T}}$, and rapidity in the centre-of-mass system, $y^{*}$. The $ψ(2S)$-to-$J/ψ$ ratio in $p\mathrm{Ar}$ collisions over that in $pp$ collisions is measured to be $0.90 \pm 0.04 \pm 0.02$ for $-2.3<y^{*}<0.0$ and $0<p_{\mathrm{T}}<8\mathrm{GeV}/c$, indicating the emergence of nuclear effects in the $p\mathrm{Ar}$ system. This study acts as a baseline for the interpretation of future measurements with larger systems accessible by the LHCb experiment.

</details>


### [148] [Characterizing Single-Signal Events from Atmospheric-Neutrino Neutral-Current Interactions in Large Liquid Scintillator Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28095)
*Zhenning Qu,Jie Cheng,Wan-lei Guo,Gaosong Li,Yu-Feng Li,Liang-jian Wen*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates atmospheric neutrino interactions in large liquid scintillator detectors, focusing on model dependence, event rates, and energy spectra of single-signal events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the potential of large liquid scintillator detectors for studying neutrino interactions and improving background estimates in rare-event searches.

Method: Systematic investigation of the model dependence of atmospheric-neutrino singles through analysis of neutrino-nucleus interactions, de-excitation, and secondary interactions in scintillators.

Result: Dominant model dependence is from primary neutrino-nucleus interactions, particularly in neutral-current processes, with informative predictions on event rates and spectra.

Conclusion: The findings support the physics potential of current and future liquid scintillator detectors in studying neutrino interactions and enhancing rare-event search methodologies.

Abstract: Neutral-current interactions of atmospheric neutrinos in large liquid scintillator detectors offer a new opportunity to study single-signal events (hereafter singles), characterized by a prompt energy deposition on the MeV-to-GeV scale and no identified delayed signal. In this work, we systematically investigate the model dependence of atmospheric-neutrino singles due to the primary neutrino-nucleus interaction, residual-nucleus de-excitation, and secondary interactions in the scintillator. Our results show that the dominant model dependence originates from the primary neutrino-nucleus interaction, especially for neutral-current processes on carbon, whereas de-excitation is essential for the singles selection yet leads to relatively small spectral variations among realistic models. Secondary-interaction effects are also subdominant overall. We further present the predicted event rates and prompt-energy spectra for neutral-current singles, along with the charged-current contribution. Separately, we estimate the low-energy contribution from elastic scattering of sub-\SI{100}{\MeV} atmospheric neutrinos on free protons. These results highlight the physics potential of current and future large liquid scintillator detectors, such as the Jiangmen Underground Neutrino Observatory, to study atmospheric-neutrino singles, probe neutrino-nucleus interaction models, and improve background estimates for rare-event searches.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [149] [Constrained functional priors for Bayesian inference of hot QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28786)
*G. Guimarães,L. Perin,M. Luzum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A nonparametric Bayesian framework using Gaussian processes improves the specification of functional priors in heavy-ion collision analyses, reducing uncertainty and enhancing inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the significant uncertainty in prior specifications of temperature-dependent transport coefficients and equations of state in Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Development of a nonparametric framework employing Gaussian processes with truncated Karhunen--Loève expansions for optimized representations, alongside methods for incorporating physical constraints.

Result: Effective incorporation of physical constraints leads to systematic and nontrivial correlations in the Bayesian inference process, revealing a clearer statistical structure in finite-dimensional representations.

Conclusion: This framework enhances the use of functional priors in heavy-ion phenomenology, providing a more robust approach to Bayesian analysis.

Abstract: Bayesian analyses of heavy-ion collisions rely on functional inputs, such as temperature-dependent transport coefficients and the equation of state, whose prior specification remains a significant source of uncertainty. Standard approaches employ low-dimensional parametrizations that can impose artificial correlations and leave the resulting inference sensitive to the assumed functional form.
  We develop a nonparametric framework for constructing priors over such functions using Gaussian processes, tailored to emulator-based inference in heavy-ion phenomenology. Truncated Karhunen--Loève expansions yield optimized finite-dimensional representations suitable for use as emulator inputs. We investigate how physical constraints can be incorporated into these priors, demonstrating that rejection-based methods produce non-Gaussian measures and induce nontrivial correlations in the expansion coefficients. By contrast, pushforward constructions enforce the constraints while preserving a tractable Gaussian measure in a latent space.
  These results provide a systematic framework for incorporating physical constraints into functional priors and clarify their effects on the statistical structure of the finite-dimensional representations used in Bayesian inference.

</details>


### [150] [First Evidence for an Unambiguous Triangle Singularity from $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29006)
*Qi Huang,Yi-Jia Zeng,Xiao-Rui Lyu,Rong-Gang Ping,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides the first experimental evidence for triangle singularities in hadronic processes by analyzing data from $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$, revealing a significant cusp-like structure in the invariant mass spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally confirm the existence of triangle singularities predicted by Landau, which have been proposed to play a role in hadronic decay processes and exotic hadrons.

Method: The authors analyze the $pη/\bar{p}η$ invariant mass spectrum from the $ψ(2S)$ decay using data from the BESIII Collaboration, fitting the data including triangle singularity loops.

Result: The analysis shows a significant cusp-like structure at 1.564 GeV consistent with triangle singularity prediction, improving fit quality significantly (reduction in $χ^2$ from 1.22 to 0.90).

Conclusion: The clear correlation between the observed structure and theoretical predictions provides compelling evidence for the existence of triangle singularities in hadronic processes.

Abstract: Triangle singularities, predicted by Landau in 1959, are purely kinematic enhancements arising from hadronic rescattering loops. Despite their proposed role in various anomalous decay processes and exotic hadron candidates, a direct experimental confirmation has remained elusive for more than six decades. We analyze the $pη/\bar{p}η$ invariant mass spectrum in $ψ(2S) \to p\bar{p}η$ measured by the BESIII Collaboration. The data exhibit a clear cusp-like structure around 1.564~GeV in the $N(1535)$ region, in precise agreement with the kinematic position predicted for the triangle singularity. Including the triangle singularity loop in the fit substantially improves the description of the data, with $χ^2/\mathrm{d.o.f.}$ decreasing from 1.22 to 0.90, corresponding to a significance of $\sim 3.8σ$ for the triangle singularity contribution. The precise alignment of the observed excess with the predicted kinematic position provides the first compelling evidence for the triangle singularity effect.

</details>


### [151] [Towards power corrections in the factorization of baryon quasi-distribution amplitudes in LaMET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29042)
*Yu-Ji Shi,Jun Zeng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically analyzes power corrections in quasi-distribution amplitudes (quasi-DAs) for baryons, establishing a relation with light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) and demonstrating the rapid decrease of target-mass corrections with increased baryon momentum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in phenomenological studies involving baryon light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) by understanding power corrections arising from quasi-DAs.

Method: The authors derive a closed-form relation between quasi-DAs and LCDAs, resumming target-mass corrections and assessing them numerically for the Λ baryon using lattice data.

Result: Target-mass corrections are found to diminish quickly with increased baryon momentum, becoming negligible in endpoint regions; next-to-leading-twist operators are constructed for quasi-DA factorization.

Conclusion: This analysis paves the way for better quantification of power corrections in lattice QCD determinations of baryon LCDAs.

Abstract: Light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) are essential to precision phenomenological studies. They can be accessed from lattice QCD through the large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) via quasi-distribution amplitudes (quasi-DAs). Factorization of quasi-DAs receive power corrections in inverse powers of the hadron momentum, including target-mass and higher-twist corrections. In this work, we present the first systematic analysis of such power corrections for the leading-twist baryon quasi-DA. Establishing the moment relation between the quasi-DA and the LCDA, we derive an exact closed-form relation that resums target-mass correction to all orders at leading twist. This result also applies to heavy baryons and to quasi-transverse-momentum-dependent distributions. We numerically assess these corrections for the $Λ$ baryon quasi-DA using existing lattice data, finding that the target-mass correction decreases rapidly with increasing baryon momentum and is almost negligible in the endpoint regions. In addition, we explicitly construct the next-to-leading-twist operators entering the quasi-DA factorization. Our results are a first step toward quantifying the power corrections in future lattice determinations of light or heavy baryon LCDAs.

</details>


### [152] [Taming lepton portal dark matter by a non-invertible selection rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29051)
*Junichiro Kawamura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores non-invertible selection rules to control flavor violation in lepton portal dark matter, presenting a model based on symmetries that aligns with the PMNS matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the flavor violation in lepton portal dark matter, which is a candidate for weakly interacting massive particles that can avoid direct detection restrictions.

Method: A construction using $	ext{Z}_2$ gauging of a $	ext{Z}_5$ symmetry is proposed to achieve one-flavor dominance in portal couplings, aligning with the PMNS matrix's large mixing angles.

Result: The model successfully demonstrates suppression of flavor-violating portal couplings while accounting for the PMNS matrix, alongside assessing flavor leakage in type-I seesaw scenarios.

Conclusion: Experimental probes suggest that the proposed model can be tested through lepton observables, particularly in the context of lepton flavor non-universality in $Z$ boson decays.

Abstract: We point out that a non-invertible selection rule can control the flavor violation inherent in lepton portal dark matter, a weakly interacting massive particle candidate that can evade severe constraints from direct detection experiments. We show that ordinary invertible symmetries cannot suppress flavor-violating portal couplings while accommodating the large mixing angles in the PMNS matrix. We then present a simple construction based on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauging of a $\mathbb{Z}_5$ symmetry, which realizes one-flavor dominance of the portal couplings consistently with the PMNS mixing matrix. We also discuss possible flavor leakage in a type-I seesaw completion and study experimental probes of the model through lepton observables, especially lepton flavor non-universality in $Z$ boson decays.

</details>


### [153] [Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT: shape and truncation studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29132)
*Manosch Bär,Bakar Chargeishvili,Ramona Gröber,Gudrun Heinrich,Konstantin Schmid*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study examines how Wilson coefficients affect Higgs boson pair production in SMEFT up to dimension-6, revealing significant shape changes in distributions under certain conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the impact of dimension-6 Wilson coefficients on the production cross sections of Higgs boson pairs and identify benchmarks with notable shape distortions.

Method: A comparative analysis incorporating NLO QCD corrections was conducted, utilizing polynomial parametrizations of the cross section based on Wilson coefficients, focusing on differential distributions of Higgs boson mass and transverse momenta.

Result: The analysis shows that certain benchmark scenarios display significant shape distortions in the differential distributions, with some scenarios remaining valid under a linear SMEFT expansion while others lead to unphysical results when higher-order contributions are neglected.

Conclusion: The breakdown of the linear approximation in the presence of large anomalous couplings highlights the need to consider quadratic contributions in the SMEFT framework.

Abstract: We present a comparative study of the influence of Wilson coefficients on Higgs boson pair production cross sections in SMEFT up to dimension-6, also including loop-suppressed operators. Our study accounts for NLO QCD corrections, and is based on updated parametrizations of the cross section as a polynomial in the Wilson coefficients. We focus on differential distributions for the Higgs boson pair invariant mass, as well as the transverse momenta of the leading Higgs bosons, identifying benchmark scenarios exhibiting pronounced shape distortions in the $m_{hh}$ and $p_{T,h}$ distributions. While some of these scenarios remain physically meaningful under a purely linear truncation of the SMEFT expansion, others yield unphysical differential cross sections when quadratic dimension-6 contributions are omitted, exposing the breakdown of the linear approximation for large anomalous couplings.

</details>


### [154] [Probing strange-sector flavor-changing neutral currents at DUNE-like facilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29178)
*Xin-Shuai Yan,Miao Liu,Qin Chang,Ya-Dong Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study assesses DUNE-like facilities' sensitivity to flavor-changing neutral currents via neutrino interactions, focusing on quark interactions and providing estimates on sensitivity levels for various configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the potential of DUNE-like experiments in constraining flavor-changing neutral-current interactions involving down and strange quarks.

Method: Evaluated projected sensitivities using a low-energy effective Lagrangian model for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos, focusing on near and far detector configurations.

Result: Strongest constraints on Wilson coefficients reach between $	ext{O}(10^{-3})$ and $	ext{O}(10^{-2})$ at the near detector, with far-detector limits about a factor of ten weaker, significantly influenced by various uncertainties.

Conclusion: Systematic uncertainties impact sensitivity differently at the near and far detectors, with near detectors mostly affected by background-rate uncertainty and far detectors by signal-rate uncertainty; neutrino mass ordering and CP phase effects are mainly observed for electron flavor coefficients.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of DUNE-like facilities to flavor-changing
  neutral-current interactions between down and strange quarks through inclusive
  deep-inelastic neutrino scattering. In the framework of a general low-energy
  effective Lagrangian for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos, we evaluate projected
  sensitivities for CP- and $τ$-optimized beam configurations. The strongest
  bounds on the dimensionless Wilson coefficients, defined relative to the Fermi
  constant $G_F$, reach $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})\text{--}\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})$ at the
  near detector, whereas far-detector limits are roughly one order of magnitude
  weaker. When systematic uncertainties are included, the near-detector
  sensitivity is dominated by the background-rate uncertainty, while the
  far-detector sensitivity is limited primarily by the signal-rate uncertainty.
  At the far detector,
  the dependence of the projected bounds on the neutrino mass ordering and the Dirac
  CP-violating phase $δ_{\mathrm{CP}}$ is largely confined to coefficients
  involving the electron flavor.

</details>


### [155] [Embedding dependence of fermion mass hierarchies in $S_3$-symmetric Yukawa sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29259)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study examines the embedding of Yukawa sectors within symmetry representations, specifically within $S_3$ symmetry, revealing conditions for mass spectra and hierarchies in particle physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to understand how family symmetries impact Yukawa couplings and mass spectra in particle physics, particularly when irreducible representations exhibit multiplicity.

Method: Analyzing complex-symmetric three-family tensors and their properties under $S_3$ symmetry, focusing on the spectral analysis of multi-dimensional invariant Yukawa subspaces while solving the inverse singular-value problem to establish conditions on mass spectra.

Result: Establishment of necessary conditions for achieving any ordered positive mass spectrum; findings reveal that fixed embedding leads to constraints on mass hierarchies, while varying embedding supports wider spectra.

Conclusion: The limitations on mass hierarchies are linked to the specific multiplicity embedding of Yukawa tensors, rather than solely on the $S_3$ representation content.

Abstract: When irreducible representations occur with multiplicity, a family symmetry fixes the representation content of a Yukawa sector but need not fix its embedding in the space of generation tensors. We study the spectral consequences of this additional embedding data for complex-symmetric three-family tensors with the $S_3$ assignment $V=\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$. Since $\operatorname{Sym}^2V=2\,\mathbf{1}\oplus2\,\mathbf{2}$, three-dimensional invariant Yukawa subspaces of type $\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{2}$ form a continuous $\mathbb{CP}^1\times\mathbb{CP}^1$ family, denoted $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ on a finite affine chart. On the $κ=0$ branch, we solve the inverse singular-value problem and obtain necessary and sufficient conditions for any prescribed ordered positive mass spectrum. A fixed singlet embedding imposes a finite hierarchy bound, whereas varying the embedding accommodates every positive three-family spectrum. For general embeddings, $\mathcal L_{t,κ}$ contains a nonzero rank-one matrix if and only if $2tκ^2=1$; equivalently, these are precisely the finite-chart embeddings for which $σ_3/σ_2$ is unbounded. In a conventional complex-symmetric $S_3$ three-Higgs sector, the same condition becomes $y_2y_5^2=\sqrt{2}\,y_1y_3^2$, so order-one Yukawa coupling ratios can support arbitrarily large mass hierarchies. We then apply the result to a recent Clifford-algebraic construction and show that its family-resolved Yukawa tensors span $\mathcal L_{2,0}$, whose singular values obey $m_1+m_2\leq m_3\leq m_1+3m_2$. This excludes the observed charged-lepton hierarchy for arbitrary complex vacuum alignment within the existing Higgs directions. The resulting obstruction is therefore tied to the fixed multiplicity embedding rather than to the $S_3$ representation content alone.

</details>


### [156] [Probing Ultra-Compressed Scotogenic Dark Matter at the HL-LHC via 4D Spacetime Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29275)
*Renjie Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores a method for detecting ultra-compressed dark matter scenarios at the HL-LHC using timing resolution to enhance signal detection even in partially optimized conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The research is motivated by the compatibility of dark matter models with existing relic density observations and the inadequacy of current detection strategies for specific decay signals.

Method: Utilizes the 30 ps timing resolution of HL-LHC detectors to measure decay lepton times, transforming the expected decay lengths into observable time delays, with a focus on low-pT disappearing tracks.

Result: Signal efficiencies range from 12% to 36% (14% at 200 GeV) with zero background detected across 4.2 million prompt SM events, projecting 95% C.L. exclusion for scalar masses up to approximately 670 GeV under conservative assumptions.

Conclusion: The proposed 4D timing method enables the exploration of the co-annihilation corridor for dark matter detection, showing that small couplings are viable within the framework of radiative neutrino mass mechanisms.

Abstract: The ultra-compressed scotogenic regime, in which a 2\,GeV mass splitting between the charged inert scalar and the dark-matter fermion is motivated by and compatible with co-annihilation to the observed relic density for suitable choices of the remaining model parameters, produces decay leptons for which existing LHC displaced-lepton and disappearing-track searches retain only partial, unoptimised acceptance, leaving room for a dedicated timing-assisted strategy. We propose exploiting the 30\,ps timing resolution of the HL-LHC precision timing detectors as a fourth observable, converting the macroscopic scalar decay length of 100--1000\,mm into an arrival-time delay of 70--700\,ps---more than three orders of magnitude above the sub-picosecond delays of all prompt Standard Model backgrounds. Operating at the level of a low-$p_T$ disappearing-track stub matched to a delayed timing hit, without requiring full lepton reconstruction, our 4D timing strategy achieves signal efficiencies of 12--36\% across the probed mass range (14\% at the 200\,GeV benchmark) and zero background across $4.20\times10^6$ simulated prompt SM events, projecting 95\%\,C.L.\ exclusion up to scalar masses of $\simeq\!670$\,GeV at 3000\,fb$^{-1}$ under conservative background assumptions. The scalar lifetime is set by the Yukawa coupling $y$ through $cτ\propto y^{-2}$, while sub-eV neutrino masses constrain only the product $y^2λ_5$; the small couplings that place the charged scalar in the optimal timing window are therefore compatible with, though not uniquely fixed by, the radiative neutrino-mass mechanism, making the co-annihilation corridor---conventionally the most elusive regime---an accessible target for 4D spacetime tracking at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [157] [Finite-Width Dissolution of Radial Spectroscopy in Single-Top Mesonic Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29476)
*Bing-Dong Wan,Shuo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the pole width of unstable heavy quark systems, finding that the width characteristics are inherited from heavy constituents under specific conditions, notably in the context of top quark physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the properties of unstable heavy quarks, particularly the top quark, affect observable phenomena in particle physics, such as decay widths and resonance structures.

Method: The study employs a complex top pole mass within an instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter framework, using a biorthogonal Hellmann–Feynman relation for width inheritance and an artificial heavy-mass scan for confirming theoretical predictions about suppression effects.

Result: The analysis reveals a single broad maximum at the physical top width across different channels ($tar b$, $tar c$, $tar u$), and confirms that stable-top eigenvalues produce observable signatures, though not as distinct multi-peak structures.

Conclusion: The findings indicate that these heavy quark systems retain stable eigenvalue poles that influence collision processes, leading to features like broad threshold enhancements, thus enriching the interpretation of heavy quark dynamics.

Abstract: Within the heavy-mass expansion, the pole width of a system containing one unstable heavy constituent inherits the constituent width up to $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm kin}^2/m_Q^2)$ corrections, while radial splittings remain $\mathcal O(Λ_{\rm rad})$. The top quark is an extreme realization of this hierarchy. We implement the complex top pole mass in an instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter framework, where a biorthogonal Hellmann--Feynman relation realizes width inheritance at the operator level and an artificial heavy-mass scan confirms the predicted $m_Q^{-2}$ suppression. The low-pole source-projected response has a single broad maximum at the physical top width in the $t\bar b$, $t\bar c$, and $t\bar u$ channels. Full width-dependent non-Hermitian re-diagonalization and a direct full-matrix resolvent evaluation confirm the progressive dissolution of the small-width radial maxima. Thus stable-top eigenvalues survive as reference poles but not as a resolvable multi-peak spectrum; they may instead leave qualitative, process-dependent $Wb\bar q$ signatures, such as a broad threshold enhancement or modified color flow.

</details>


### [158] [Probability-based Estimates of the Uncalculated N$^5$LO QCD Contribution to the Hadronic $W$-Boson Decay Width](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29666)
*Shu-Heng Yang,Jiang Yan,Xing-Gang Wu,Zhi-Fei Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study estimates the N^5LO corrections to the W boson’s hadronic decay width using Bayesian analysis and the Principle of Maximum Conformality, achieving a credible interval that aligns well with experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of perturbative QCD corrections to the W boson decay width, particularly in light of the computational challenges of determining N^5LO corrections directly.

Method: The authors employ Bayesian analysis alongside the Principle of Maximum Conformality to refine the scale-dependent perturbative series, achieving scale invariance and improved convergence through the renormalization group equation.

Result: The Bayesian analysis yields a 95.5% credible interval for the N^5LO contributions, resulting in a hadronic branching ratio of (65.84±1.54)%, consistent with experimental results.

Conclusion: The study provides a refined estimate of the W boson’s hadronic decay width, enhancing theoretical predictions and aligning them closely with experimental findings.

Abstract: The perturbative QCD corrections to the hadronic decay width of the $W$ boson are currently known up to next-to-next-to-next-to-next-to-leading order ($\mathrm{N^4LO}$), whereas the exact $\mathrm{N^5LO}$ correction remains unavailable owing to its formidable computational complexity. In this work, we estimate the $\mathrm{N^5LO}$ contributions by adopting Bayesian analysis (BA). Before performing the estimation, the Principle of Maximum Conformality (PMC) is employed to improve the precision of the initial scale-dependent perturbative series. Through recursive application of the renormalization group equation, non-conformal terms are absorbed into the strong running coupling, yielding a scheme-independent, scale-invariant perturbative series with improved convergence. The PMC procedure determines an effective coupling $α_s(Q_*)$, with the PMC scale fixed as $Q_* = 100.102~\mathrm{GeV}$ at next-to-next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy. Based on the improved and more precise series, the $95.5\%$ BA credible interval yields an uncertainty of $Δδ_\mathrm{QCD}|_{\mathrm{PMC, BA}}^{\mathrm{N^5LO}} = \pm 1.0\times 10^{-5}$. The resulting hadronic branching ratio is $\mathcal{B}(W\to \text{hadrons})|_\mathrm{PMC} = (65.84\pm 1.54)\%$, which is consistent with experimental data within reasonable errors.

</details>


### [159] [On-shell renormalization in NREFT: a simplified description of resonances, bound states, and coupled channel effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29683)
*Guo-Ying Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the modern application of Weinberg's compositeness criterion, specifically through on-shell renormalization in NREFT, while discussing resonances and coupled channel effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address and clarify issues surrounding the generalization of Weinberg's compositeness criterion in modern theoretical frameworks.

Method: Utilizing on-shell renormalization in NREFT to demonstrate the applicability of Weinberg's criterion and to describe resonances and bound states.

Result: Verification that on-shell renormalization accurately reproduces Weinberg's result and effectively describes coupled channel effects and resonances.

Conclusion: The study provides a complete propagator analysis, including charged channels for p-wave states near threshold, enhancing understanding of the compositeness criterion.

Abstract: We discuss several issues regarding the modern generalization of Weinberg's compositeness criterion. In particular, we demonstrate that the on-shell renormalization in NREFT reproduces Weinberg's result and provides a simple description of resonances, bound states, and coupled channel effects. Additionally, we present the complete propagator, including the charged channel for the near-threshold p-wave states.

</details>


### [160] [$(g-2)_{\ell}$ and LFV decays in a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29697)
*L. T. Hue,Vo Quoc Phong,T. D. Tham,N. H. T. Nha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses a left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos, showing how heavy singly charged Higgs bosons can address the discrepancies in charged lepton anomalous magnetic moments, while lepton-flavor violating decay rates remain suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to explore a left-right model that could explain the observed anomalies in the anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons, specifically the electron and muon.

Method: The authors analyze one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons within the framework of a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ symmetry model.

Result: The study finds that the contributions can account for the observed discrepancies in the magnetic moments of the electron and muon, with results of $10^{-9}$ for the muon and $	ext{O}(10^{-13})$ for the electron.

Conclusion: The lepton-flavor violating decay rates are significantly suppressed due to the imposed $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ symmetry, suggesting a viable model that addresses current discrepancies while maintaining lepton flavor integrity.

Abstract: In the framework of a $U(1)_{L_μ -L_τ}$ left-right model with inverse seesaw neutrinos recently proposed, we show that one-loop contributions from heavy singly charged Higgs bosons to the anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons $(g-2)_{e,μ}$ can accommodate the observed discrepancies, of $10^{-9}$ and $\mathcal{O}(10^{-13})$ for the muon and electron, respectively. Meanwhile, all lepton-flavor violating decay rates for $h\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp $, $Z\to e_b^\pm e_a^\mp$, and $e_b\to e_a γ$ are found to be highly suppressed as a consequence of the gauged $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ symmetry.

</details>
